is not stable, a contradiction. It follows that
\TilTheta(M)\!=\!\mathcal{F}(1)
is stable. ∎

We summarize our findings in the following

Theorem 6.6.3
.

Let
M\in\EKP(K_{3})\!\smallsetminus\!\{0\}
.

(1)

If
\Delta_{M}(2)\!\geq\!0
, then
\TilTheta(M)
is semistable if and only if
M
is semistable.

(2)

If
\Delta_{M}(2)\!>\!0
, then
\TilTheta(M)
is stable if and only if
M
is stable.

Proof.

This is just a combination of Proposition
6.1.1
and Proposition
6.6.2
. ∎

Examples
.

(a) Exceptional Steiner bundles. According to
[
35
, (4.1)]
, exceptional vector bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{2}(\mathbb{C})
are stable. Thanks to
[
14
, (1.1)]
, this also holds for exceptional Steiner bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}(\mathbb{C})
. Returning to our context, we let
\mathcal{F}
be an exceptional Steiner bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. According to Proposition
4.2.1
, there is a preprojective indecomposable representation
M\in\EKP(K_{3})
such that

\mathcal{F}\cong\TilTheta(M)
. A consecutive application of Proposition
2.2.1
and Theorem
2.3.2
ensures that
\Delta_{M}(2)\!>\!0
. Now Theorem
6.6.3
in conjunction with the remark
following Corollary
6.2.2
shows that
\mathcal{F}
is stable.

(b) ”Regular” indecomposable Steiner bundles. Let
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a pair of
\mathbbm{k}
-vector spaces such that
\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}(2)\!>\!0
and
q_{3}(\udim(V_{1},V_{2}))\!<\!0
. The stable representations of
\rep(K_{3};V_{1},V_{2})\cap\EKP(K_{3})
may
be interpreted as the stable points of the variety
\mathbb{P}(\rep(K_{3};V_{1},V_{2}))
relative to the canonical action of

\SL(V_{2})\!\times\!\SL(V_{1})
. This can be used to show that the subset
\mathcal{S}(V_{1},V_{2})
of stable representations is non-empty open (and hence dense) whenever
\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}\!\geq\!2
; see also Proposition
6.8.1
below for the case, where
\mathbbm{k}\!=\!\mathbb{C}
.

6.7.
Applications: Filtrations for Steiner bundles

Suppose that
M\in\EKP(K_{3})
is semistable. If
P_{0}(r)
is a direct summand of
M
, then a two-fold application of the See-Saw Lemma implies
M_{1}\!=\!(0)
, whence
M\cong nP_{0}(r)
. Alternatively
\psi_{M}
is surjective, whence
\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}M_{2}\!\leq\!3\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}M_{1}
. Hence the conditions of Theorem
6.6.3
may seem to limit its utility. The results of this section illustrate that a combination of Theorem
6.6.3
with techniques involving AR-theory provides many examples of stable Steiner bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Theorem 6.7.1
.

Let
E\in\EKP(K_{3})
be an elementary representation. Then
\TilTheta(E)
is a stable.

Proof.

Thanks to Proposition
6.2.3
, the representation
E
is stable and
E\in\EKP(K_{3})\!\smallsetminus\!\{0\}
implies
\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{1}\!<\!\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{2}
. In view of Theorem
6.6.3
, it therefore
suffices to consider the case, where
\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{1}\!<\!\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{2}\!\leq\!2\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}E_{1}
. Thanks to
[
57
, Thm.]
,
\udim E
belongs to one of the
\sigma_{3}
-orbits generated by the dimension vectors

(1,1),(2,2)
. Since
\Delta_{\sigma_{K_{3}}(M)}\!=\!\Delta_{M}(2)\!<\!\Delta_{M}
for every regular indecomposable
M\in\rep(K_{3})
, there is a unique dimension vector
(x,y)\in\langle\sigma_{3}\rangle.\udim E
such that

\Delta_{(x,y)}\!=\!y\!-\!x\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Delta_{(x,y)}(2)\!=\!y\!-\!2x\!\leq\!0
. Hence the only dimension vectors in these orbits that satisfy
x\!<\!y\!\leq\!2x
are
(1,2)
and
(2,4)
. By virtue of Theorem
2.3.2
,
representations with dimension vector
(1,2)
do not belong to
\EKP(K_{3})
, whence
\udim E\!=\!(2,4)
. As elementary representations are bricks,
\TilTheta(E)
is a simple vector bundle of rank
2
. We conclude with

[
53
, Chap.2.(1.2.10)]
, which holds in arbitrary characteristic (cf. also
[
59
, Prop.1]
), that
\TilTheta(E)
is stable. ∎

Let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Gamma(K_{r})
be a regular Auslander-Reiten component. We say that
\mathcal{C}
is
(semi)stable
, provided
\mathcal{C}\cap\EKP(K_{r})
contains a (semi)stable vertex
21
21

21

Recall from
6.2.2
(3) that semistable vertices are quasi-simple.
. The following result
shows that for such components the Harder-Narasimhan filtrations of most of the bundles
\TilTheta(M)
correspond to the quasi-composition series of
M
.

Proposition 6.7.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Gamma(K_{3})
be a semistable AR-component. If
M\in(\tau^{-1}_{K_{3}}(M_{\mathcal{C},1})\!\rightarrow)
has quasi-length
\ell
, then the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\TilTheta(M)
is given by

(0)\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[1]})\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[2]})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[\ell\!-\!1]})\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[\ell]})=\TilTheta(M)

with filtration factors
\TilTheta(M_{[j]})/\TilTheta(M_{[j\!-\!1]})\cong\TilTheta(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}]))
for all
j\in\{2,\ldots,\ell\}
.

Proof.

We consider the quasi-composition series

(0)\subsetneq M_{[1]}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq M_{[\ell\!-\!1]}\subsetneq M

of
M
. For
1\!<\!j\!\leq\!\ell
general AR-theory provides an exact sequence

(0)\longrightarrow M_{[j-1]}\longrightarrow M_{[j]}\longrightarrow\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]})\longrightarrow(0)

in
\EKP(K_{3})
. According to Corollary
3.2.4
, application of
\TilTheta
yields an exact sequence

(0)\longrightarrow\TilTheta(M_{[j-1]})\longrightarrow\TilTheta(M_{[j]})\longrightarrow\TilTheta(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}))\longrightarrow(0)

so that
\TilTheta(M_{[j]})/\TilTheta(M_{[j-1]})\cong\TilTheta(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}))
. Our assumption on

M
implies that the quasi socle
M_{[1]}
belongs to the cone
(\tau^{-1}_{K_{3}}(M_{\mathcal{C},1})\!\rightarrow)
. It
follows from Theorem
2.2.3
(3) that
\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]})\in\rep(K_{3},2)
, whence
\Delta_{\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M{[1]})}(2)\!\geq\!0
.

As
\mathcal{C}
is semistable, Corollary
6.2.2
ensures that the same is true for
\tau^{-n}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]})
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We apply Theorem
6.6.3
and conclude that

\TilTheta(M_{[j]})/\TilTheta(M_{[j-1]})
is semistable. Proposition
6.2.1
(3) yields

\mu(\TilTheta(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]})))=\mu(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}))>\mu(\tau^{-j}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}))=\mu(\TilTheta(\tau^{-j}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]})))

for all
1\!\leq\!j\!\leq\!\ell
. The statement now follows from the uniqueness of the Harder-Narasimhan filtration. ∎

Proposition 6.7.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Gamma(K_{3})
be a regular component. If
M\in\mathcal{C}\cap\repp(K_{3},d)
has minimal type, then
\TilTheta(N)
is stable for every quasi-simple
N\in(M_{\mathcal{C},d}\!\rightarrow)
.

Proof.

Corollary
2.3.3
and Proposition
4.3.4
show that
M\!=\!M_{\mathcal{C},d}
is a brick. If
d\!=\!2
, then Lemma
6.1.2
and Theorem
6.6.3
imply that
\TilTheta(M)
is stable. Alternatively,

\TilTheta(M)\in\Vect(\mathbb{P}^{2})
is simple and of rank
2
, so that
[
53
, Chap.2,(1.2.10)]
implies the stability of
\TilTheta(M)
. As
N\in\{M_{\mathcal{C},1}\}\cup(M_{\mathcal{C},2}\!\rightarrow)
, it follows from Corollary

6.2.2
that
\TilTheta(N)

is stable. ∎

Theorem 6.7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Gamma(K_{3})
be a regular AR-component,
\mathcal{F}
be an indecomposable Steiner bundle.

(1)

If
\mathcal{C}
contains an elementary representation and
\mathcal{F}\in\TilTheta(M_{\mathcal{C},1}\!\rightarrow)
, then
\mathcal{F}
possesses a Harder-Narasimhan filtration by Steiner bundles, whose filtration factors are stable.

(2)

If
\mathcal{F}
has a resolution

(0)\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1)^{a}\to\mathcal{O}^{b}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\to\mathcal{F}\to(0)

such that
(a,b)
is an imaginary root and
q_{3}(a,b)\!+\!\Delta_{(a,b)}(2)\!\geq\!1
, then
\mathcal{F}
possesses a filtration

(0)=\mathcal{F}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}=\mathcal{F}

by Steiner bundles such that each quotient
\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
is a stable Steiner bundle.

(3)

Suppose
X\in\mathcal{C}
is quasi-simple such that
q_{3}(\udim X)\!+\!\Delta_{X}(2)\!\geq\!1
. If
\mathcal{F}\in\TilTheta(X\!\rightarrow)
, then
\mathcal{F}
possesses a
filtration

(0)=\mathcal{F}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}=\mathcal{F}

by Steiner bundles such that each quotient
\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
is a stable Steiner bundle.

Proof.

(1) Let
E\in\mathcal{C}
be elementary. By definition,
E
is quasi-simple, so that, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the module
\tau_{K_{3}}^{-n}(M_{\mathcal{C},1})
is elementary and contained
\EKP(K_{3})
. Given
\mathcal{F}\!:=\!\TilTheta(M)\in\TilTheta(M_{\mathcal{C},1}\!\rightarrow)
, we consider the quasi-composition series

(0)=M_{[0]}\subsetneq M_{[1]}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq M_{[\ql(M)]}=M,

whose filtration factors belong to
\{\tau_{K_{3}}^{-\ell}(M_{\mathcal{C},1})\ ;\ \ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
. Application of
\TilTheta
provides a filtration

(0)=\TilTheta(M_{[0]})\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[1]})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{[\ql(M)]})=\mathcal{F},

with filtration factors
\TilTheta(M_{[j]})/\TilTheta(M_{[j-1]})\cong\TilTheta(\tau^{-j+1}_{K_{3}}(M_{[1]}))
. Thanks to Theorem
6.7.1
, the factors are stable and with strictly decreasing slopes (cf. Proposition
6.2.1
), so
that we have found the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\mathcal{F}
.

(2) By Theorem
3.2.3
we can find an indecomposable representation
M\in\EKP(K_{3})
such that
\TilTheta(M)\cong\mathcal{F}
. We have
b\!-\!a\!=\!\rk(\mathcal{F})\!=\!\rk(\TilTheta(M))\!=\!\Delta_{M}
and
a\!=\!c_{1}(\mathcal{F})\!=\!c_{1}(\TilTheta(M))\!=\!\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}M_{1}
. Hence

q_{3}(\udim M)\!+\!\Delta_{M}(2)=q_{3}(a,b)\!+\!\Delta_{(a,b)}(2)\geq 1.

We conclude with Lemma
6.3.2
that there is a filtration

(0)=M_{0}\subsetneq M_{1}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq M_{n}=M

such that each quotient
M_{i}/M_{i-1}
is an elementary representation in
\EKP(K_{3})
. Exactness of
\TilTheta
on
\EKP(K_{3})
yields a filtration

(0)=\TilTheta(M_{0})\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{1})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq\TilTheta(M_{n})=\TilTheta(M)\cong\mathcal{F}

of Steiner bundles. By Theorem
6.7.1
, each quotient
\TilTheta(M_{i})/\TilTheta(M_{i-1})\cong\TilTheta(M_{i}/M_{i-1})
is a stable Steiner bundle.

(3) This follows as in (2), using Corollary
6.3.3
. ∎

6.8.
Applications: Restrictions to hyperplanes

We assume throughout that
\mathbbm{k}\!=\!\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a stable (respectively, semistable) vector bundle of rank
n
on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
with
r\!\geq\!4
. In
[
37
, Problem 5]
it was asked whether for almost all linear
hyperplanes
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
to
H
is stable (respectively, semistable).

The question was answered affirmatively by Barth
[
6
]
for
n\!=\!2
and for
n\!<\!r\!-\!1
by Maruyama
[
49
]
(in both cases with one exception). In the context of Steiner bundles, these rank conditions
imply that only (direct sums of) the structure sheaf of
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
can occur.

Consider the tangent bundle
\mathcal{T}(-1)
of rank
r\!-\!1
. Then
\mathcal{T}(-1)\cong\TilTheta(P_{1}(r))
is stable. On the other hand,
P_{1}(r\!-\!1)\subseteq\alpha^{\ast}(P_{1}(r))
for all
\alpha\in\Inj_{\mathbbm{k}}(A_{r-1},A_{r})
, while
\mu(\alpha^{\ast}(P_{1}(r)))\!=\!\frac{1}{r-1}\!<\!\frac{1}{r-2}\!=\!\mu(P_{1}(r\!-\!1))
. Thanks to Lemma
5.1.1
and Proposition
6.1.1
,
\mathcal{T}(-1)|_{H}
is not semistable for any hyperplane

H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
. This example, which does not depend on the ground field, is well-known (cf.
[
30
]
). Building on recent work of Coskun, Huizenga and Smith
[
21
]
, we will show below that generic Steiner bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
behave similarly.

Proposition 6.8.1
.

Let
r\!\geq\!3
,
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a pair of vector spaces such that

(a)

\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}\!\geq\!r\!-\!1
, and

(b)

q_{r}(\udim(V_{1},V_{2}))\!<\!1
.

There exists a non-empty open subset
O\subseteq\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})\cap\EKP(K_{r})
such that for every
M\in O
the Steiner bundle
\TilTheta(M)
is stable.

Proof.

Following
[
21
]
, we define
\psi_{r-1}:=\frac{r-2+\sqrt{(r-1)^{2}+2(r-1)-3}}{2}
, so that

\psi_{r-1}\!+\!1=\frac{r+\sqrt{(r\!-\!1)^{2}\!+\!2(r\!-\!1)\!-\!3}}{2}=\frac{r\!+\!\sqrt{r^{2}\!-\!4}}{2}=L_{r}.

As
V_{1}\!\neq\!(0)
, our current assumptions imply

\frac{r\!-\!1}{\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}}\leq\frac{\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}}{\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \frac{\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}}{\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}}=\frac{\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{2}}{\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}}\!-\!1<L_{r}\!-\!1=\psi_{r-1},

respectively. Thanks to
[
21
, (5.1)]
, there is a dense open subset

O\subseteq\Hom_{\mathbb{P}^{r-1}}(V_{1}\!\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{r-1}}(-1),V_{2}\!\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{r-1}})\cong\Hom_{\mathbbm{k}}(V_{1},V_{2})^{r}=\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})

such that
O\subseteq\EKP(K_{r})
, and for every
M\in O
the Steiner bundle
\TilTheta(M)
is stable. ∎

Remark
.

The statement is also true if
q_{r}(\udim(V_{1},V_{2}))\!=\!1
and
\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}\!\leq\!\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{2}
. In this case there exists a non-empty open subset
O\subseteq\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
such that every
representation in
O
is isomorphic to some
P_{i}(r)
. By Proposition
4.2.1
and
[
14
, (8.1)]
, the bundles
\TilTheta(P_{i}(r))
are stable.
22
22

22

In
[
14
]
Gieseker stability was considered. It can be
shown, however, that the relevant result of loc. cit. also holds in our context.

Corollary 6.8.2
.

Assume that
q_{r}(\udim(V_{1},V_{2}))\!<\!1
and
\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}(r\!-\!1)\!\geq\!r\!-\!1
. A general Steiner bundle
\mathcal{F}
of rank
\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}
and with first Chern class
\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}
is stable and
\mathcal{F}|_{H}
is not semistable for each hyperplane
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.

Proof.

Let
O\subseteq\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
be the non-empty open set defined in Proposition
6.8.1
. By Proposition
2.2.4
the set
O\cap\repp(K_{r},r\!-\!1)
is non-empty and open. Let
M\in O\cap\repp(K_{r},r\!-\!1)
, then
\mathcal{F}\!:=\!\TilTheta(M)
is stable. Since
M\in\repp(K_{r},r\!-\!1)
, we have
\alpha^{\ast}(M)\cong\Delta_{M}(r\!-\!1)P_{0}(r\!-\!1)\!\oplus\!(\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}M_{1})P_{1}(r\!-\!1)
for all
\alpha\in\Inj_{\mathbbm{k}}(A_{r-1},A_{r})
. Thus,
\mu(\alpha^{\ast}(M))\!<\!\mu(P_{1}(r\!-\!1))
, and the above arguments show that for every hyperplane
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}|_{H}
is not semistable. ∎

Corollary
6.8.2
provides negative answers to
[
37
, Problem 5]
for a fixed rank and Chern class on an open subset of
\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
.

Our next result shows that we can find an infinite family of Steiner bundles of fixed rank and Chern class that are counter examples and even belong to different
\GL(A_{r})
-orbits. Moreover, these bundles are
isomorphic when we restrict them to any hyperplane.

Corollary 6.8.3
.

Let
(V_{1},V_{2})
be a pair of vector spaces such that

(a)

q_{r}(\udim(V_{1},V_{1}))\!<\!-r^{2}
, and

(b)

\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}(r\!-\!1)\!\geq\!r\!-\!1
.

There exists an infinite family
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
of Steiner bundles such that for all
i,j\in I
the following statements hold:

(1)

\mathcal{F}_{i}
is stable.

(2)

c(\mathcal{F}_{i})\!=\!\dim_{\mathbbm{k}}V_{1}
,
\rk(\mathcal{F}_{i})\!=\!\Delta_{(V_{1},V_{2})}
.

(3)

\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{H}\cong\mathcal{F}_{j}|_{H}
for each hyperplane
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.

(4)

\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{H}
is not semistable for each hyperplane
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.

(5)

All
\mathcal{F}_{i}
lie in different orbits under the natural action of
\GL(A_{r})
on
\Coh(\mathbb{P}^{r-1})
.

Proof.

Let
O\subseteq\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
be the non-empty open set defined in Proposition
6.8.1
. The algebraic group
G\!:=\GL(A_{r})\!\times\!\GL(V_{2})\!\times\!\GL(V_{1})
acts canonically on

\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})\cong\Hom_{\mathbbm{k}}(A_{r}\!\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}\!V_{1},V_{2})
. We consider the
G
-stable and open set
U\!:=\!\bigcup_{g\in G}g.O\subseteq\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
and set
\mathcal{U}\!:=\!U\cap\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})\cap\repp(K_{r},r\!-\!1)
. By Proposition
2.2.4
the
G
-stable set
\mathcal{U}
is non-empty and open.

Let
M\in\mathcal{U}
. We find
(a,h_{2},h_{1})\in G
and
S\in O\cap\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})\cap\repp(K_{r},r\!-\!1)
such that
M\!=\!(a,h_{2},h_{1}).S
. By definition, the representations
M
and
(a,\id_{V_{2}},\id_{V_{1}}).S\!=\!S^{(a)}
are isomorphic. We obtain

\mathcal{F}:=\TilTheta(M)\cong\TilTheta(S^{(a)})\cong a^{\ast}.\TilTheta(S),

see Section
5.1
. Since
S\in O
, we know that
\TilTheta(S)
is stable. Therefore, the same is true for
a^{\ast}.\TilTheta(S)\cong\mathcal{F}
. This shows that every bundle arising in this way has property (1). Owing to Theorem
3.2.3
and
Corollary
3.3.2
, each bundle of this form satisfies (2). The arguments in the proof of Corollary
6.8.2
now show that (3) and (4) hold. Since
q_{r}(\udim(V_{1},V_{2}))\!<\!-r^{2}
we can apply
[
10
, (4.2)]
and
conclude that
\mathcal{U}/G
is not finite. By construction, elements of
\rep(K_{r};V_{1},V_{2})
belonging to different
G
-orbits correspond to Steiner bundles, whose isomorphisms classes belong to different orbits under the
action of
\GL(A_{r})
. This shows (5). ∎

Examples
.

We fix
r\!=\!4
.

(1)
q_{r}(5,18)\!=\!-11
and
\Delta_{(5,18)}(3)\!=\!3
. We apply Corollary
6.8.2
and find a stable Steiner bundle of rank
13
on
\mathbb{P}^{3}
such that
\mathcal{F}|_{H}
is not semistable for each hyperplane

H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{3}
.

(2)
q_{r}(6,21)\!=\!-27\!<\!-4^{2}
and
\Delta_{(6,21)}(3)\!=\!3
. We apply Corollary
6.8.3
and find an infinite family of stable Steiner bundles
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i\in I}
of rank
15
on
\mathbb{P}^{3}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{H}
is not
semistable for all
i\in I
and all hyperplanes
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{3}
. Moreover, we have
\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{H}\cong\mathcal{F}_{j}|_{H}
for each hyperplane and all
i,j\in I
, while
\mathcal{F}_{i}
are
\mathcal{F}_{j}
are not in the same
\GL(A_{r})
-orbit.
